  "prior_findings": [
    {
      "action": "Preserve both actions or advance the checkpoint state only when the checkpoint is actually delivered. Add a regression test for a third or fourth loop completion that also resolves a deferred request.",
      "evidence": [
        "In `ProgressRule._on_complete`, a repeated command completing after a deferred check sets `_request` to `delivered-between-checks` and `_pending` to the checkpoint action.",
        "For the third or fourth identical test, lint, or typecheck completion, the loop branch overwrites `_pending` with its own message. `on_event` delivers only that final action.",
        "The next empty check treats `delivered-between-checks` as an actual checkpoint delivery and starts its window; the lane can therefore be stopped for an unanswered checkpoint it never received.",
        "The added C-B1 test uses `cat a.py`, outside the loop families, so it does not cover this overlap."
      ],
      "id": "C-B1-loop-action-overwrites-checkpoint",
      "severity": "high",
      "summary": "A loop message can replace a deferred checkpoint request while the rule still records that checkpoint as delivered."
    },
    {
      "action": "Name all in-flight commands that may be interrupted, or make the message and spec explicitly warn that any running commands may be interrupted and have partial effects.",
      "evidence": [
        "`ProgressRule._in_flight` tracks multiple commands, while `on_tick` selects only `list(in_flight.values())[-1]` for the interruption warning.",
        "`_kill_recorded_process_group` kills the executor's process group, which can include other running commands.",
        "The spec says the interruption message names the command and warns about its partial effects; the generated request tells the lane not to simply rerun the named command. Other interrupted commands can have unreported partial effects."
      ],
      "id": "parallel-command-interrupt-not-disclosed",
      "severity": "high",
      "summary": "A window-triggered preemption can interrupt multiple in-flight commands, but the checkpoint message identifies only one."
    }
  ],
  "prior_lens": "release lock-in final pass",
  "prior_next_move": "Hold release lock-in until checkpoint delivery state is corrected for overlapping loop actions and the interruption warning covers concurrent commands; add focused regression coverage.",

see above.\n    def _runtime_bootstrap_module(repo_root: Path):\n        \"\"\"Load the single runtime-environment owner without writing startup bytecode.\"\"\"\n        candidates = [repo_root.resolve()]\n        if _bootstrap.EMBEDDED_REPO_ROOT is not None:\n            candidates.append(_bootstrap.EMBEDDED_REPO_ROOT)\n        candidates.append(managed_checkout_root(default_home_root()))\n        source_root = next(\n            (\n                candidate\n                for candidate in candidates\n                if (candidate / \"scripts\" / \"runtime_bootstrap.py\").is_file()\n            ),\n            None,\n        )\n        if source_root is None:\n            return None  # pragma: no cover - needs a scriptless repo with an unembedded entry and no managed checkout; measured runs return EMBEDDED_REPO_ROOT above.\n    def _source_checkout_under_work(args: argparse.Namespace) -> Path | None:\n        \"\"\"The parent repo itself, when it is a charness source checkout.\n\n        `charness task run --repo-root .` inside this repository must run THIS\n        checkout's task runner, not the managed checkout under `~/.agents/src`:\n        on 2026-09-02 a runner fix (the worktree `.agents/` sandbox grant) sat\n        unused for a session because the installed CLI loaded the stale managed\n        copy. An explicit `--charness-checkout` still wins; a parent that is not a\n        source checkout falls through to the managed resolution.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Called only from the unembedded-only resolver above, so the whole body\n        # is likewise unreachable in measured runs.\n        parent = getattr(args, \"repo_root\", None)  # pragma: no cover - unembedded-only caller; see above.\n        if parent is None:  # pragma: no cover - unembedded-only caller; see above.\n            return None\n        candidate = Path(parent).resolve()  # pragma: no cover - unembedded-only caller; see above.\n        if (candidate / \"packaging\" / f\"{PACKAGE_ID}.json\").is_file() and (  # pragma: no cover - unembedded-only caller; see above.\n            candidate / \"scripts\" / \"task_run\" / \"task_run.py\"\n        ).is_file():\n            return candidate\n        return None  # pragma: no cover - unembedded-only caller; see above.\n\n    def bool_env(name: str) -> bool:\n        # The FIFTH copy of the table owned by `scripts/core/env_bypass.py`, and the one\n        # copy that cannot import its owner. This file is the installed standalone\n        # CLI: `source_root` above returns None when no charness source tree is\n        # present, so `scripts` is not importable here in the case this entry point\n        # exists to serve. The duplication is deliberate and load-bearing.\n        #\n        # It is still the same contract, so it keeps the same allowlist: bare\n        # truthiness would make `CHARNESS_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=0` -- the spelling an\n        # operator uses to keep the check ON -- switch it off. If the table in\n        # `scripts/core/env_bypass.py` ever changes, change it here too.\n        return os.environ.get(name, \"\").strip().lower() in {\"1\", \"true\", \"yes\", \"on\"}\n\n    def build_self_update_notice(latest_release: object) -> str | None:\n        if not isinstance(latest_release, dict):\n            return None\n        if (\n            latest_release.get(\"status\") != \"ok\"\n            or latest_release.get(\"update_available\") is not True\n        ):\n            return None\n        current_version = latest_release.get(\"current_version\") or \"unknown\"\n        latest = latest_release.get(\"latest_tag\") or latest_release.get(\"latest_version\")\n        if not isinstance(latest, str) or not latest:\n            return None\n        url = latest_release.get(\"html_url\")\n        suffix = f\" {url}\" if isinstance(url, str) and url else \"\"\n        return (\n            f\"charness release available: `{current_version}` -> `{latest}`. \"\n            f\"Upgrade with `charness update` from the installed CLI.{suffix}\"\n        )\n\n    def build_version_payload(\n        *,\n        home_root: Path,\n        repo_root: Path,\n        managed_checkout: bool,\n        cli_path: Path,\n        check: bool,\n    ) -> dict[str, object]:\n        provenance = build_version_provenance(\n            home_root=home_root,\n            repo_root=repo_root,\n            managed_checkout=managed_checkout,\n            cli_path=cli_path,\n        )\n        state = write_version_state(home_root, provenance=provenance)\n        if check:\n            state = refresh_self_release_state(\n                home_root=home_root,\n                current_version=provenance.get(\"current_version\")\n                if isinstance(provenance.get(\"current_version\"), str)\n                else None,\n            )\n        latest_release = state.get(\"latest_release\")\n        payload = {\n            \"package_id\": PACKAGE_ID,\n            \"current_version\": provenance.get(\"current_version\"),\n            \"current_git_head\": provenance.get(\"current_git_head\"),\n            \"repo_root\": str(repo_root),\n            \"managed_checkout\": managed_checkout,\n            \"version_state_path\": str(default_version_state_path(home_root)),\n            \"version_provenance\": provenance,\n            \"latest_release_check\": latest_release,\n            \"update_notice\": build_self_update_notice(latest_release),\n        }\n        return payload\n\n    def build_version_provenance(\n        *,\n        home_root: Path,\n        repo_root: Path,\n        managed_checkout: bool,\n        cli_path: Path,\n    ) -> dict[str, object]:\n        runtime_path = _bootstrap.SCRIPT_PATH.resolve()\n        configured_cli_path = cli_path.resolve()\n        checkout_present = has_source_manifest(repo_root)\n        invocation_kind = \"custom-cli\"\n        install_method: str | None = None\n\n        if runtime_path == configured_cli_path:\n            invocation_kind = \"installed-cli\"\n            install_method = \"managed-local-cli\" if managed_checkout else \"custom-cli\"\n        elif checkout_present and runtime_path == (repo_root / \"charness\").resolve():\n            invocation_kind = \"source-checkout\"\n            install_method = \"checkout\"\n\n        eligible = bool(\n            invocation_kind == \"installed-cli\"\n            and managed_checkout\n            and checkout_present\n            and packaging_version(repo_root)\n        )\n\n        return {\n            \"checked_at\": now_iso(),\n            \"repo_root\": str(repo_root),\n            \"managed_checkout\": managed_checkout,\n            \"checkout_present\": checkout_present,\n            \"current_version\": packaging_version(repo_root) if checkout_present else None,\n            \"current_git_head\": git_head(repo_root) if checkout_present else None,\n            \"runtime_path\": str(runtime_path),\n            \"configured_cli_path\": str(configured_cli_path),\n            \"invocation_kind\": invocation_kind,\n            \"install_method\": install_method,\n            \"eligible_for_auto_update_check\": eligible,\n        }\n    def compare_semver_like(left: object, right: object) -> int | None:\n        parsed_left = parse_semver_like(left)\n        parsed_right = parse_semver_like(right)\n        if parsed_left is None or parsed_right is None:\n            return None\n        left_core, left_prerelease = parsed_left\n        right_core, right_prerelease = parsed_right\n        max_length = max(len(left_core), len(right_core))\n        left_core_padded = left_core + (0,) * (max_length - len(left_core))\n        right_core_padded = right_core + (0,) * (max_length - len(right_core))\n        if left_core_padded < right_core_padded:\n            return -1\n        if left_core_padded > right_core_padded:\n            return 1\n        if left_prerelease == right_prerelease:\n            return 0\n        if not left_prerelease:\n            return 1\n        if not right_prerelease:\n            return -1\n        if left_prerelease < right_prerelease:\n            return -1\n        return 1\n\n    def compute_update_available(\n        current_version: str | None, latest_version: object\n    ) -> bool | None:\n        comparison = compare_semver_like(current_version, latest_version)\n        if comparison is None:\n            return None\n        return comparison < 0\n\n    def default_bootstrap_repo_url(home_root: Path, configured_repo_url: str) -> str:\n        return configured_repo_url\n\n    def default_claude_wrapper_path(home_root: Path) -> default_state_root(home_root) / \"version-state.json\"\n\n    def emit_progress(message: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Keep progress out of the public structured stdout stream.\"\"\"\n        print(message, file=sys.stderr, flush=True)\n\n    def emit_yaml(payload: object) -> None:\n        print(render_yaml(payload), end=\"\")\n\n    def enforce_managed_cli_contract(\n        *,\n        home_root: Path,\n        repo_root: Path,\n        managed_checkout: bool,\n        skip_cli_install: bool,\n    ) -> None:\n        if managed_checkout:\n            return\n        if skip_cli_install:\n            return\n        raise CharnessError(\n            \"official charness installs must use the managed checkout \"\n            f\"`{managed_checkout_root(home_root)}`; for proof-only runs from another checkout, rerun with `--skip-cli-install`.\"\n        )\n\n    def ensure_checkout(\n        repo_root: Path, *, managed: bool, repo_url: str, allow_clone: bool, allow_pull: bool\n    ) -> dict[str, object]:\n        if has_source_manifest(repo_root):\n            cloned = False\n            pulled = False\n            if allow_pull and managed:\n                if not is_git_checkout(repo_root):\n                    raise CharnessError(\n                        f\"`{repo_root}` contains a source manifest but is not a git checkout\"\n                    )\n                if git_has_tracked_changes(repo_root):\n                    raise CharnessError(\n                        f\"managed checkout `{repo_root}` has tracked local changes; stop and resolve them before `charness update`\"\n                    )\n                pull_result = run([\"git\", \"pull\", \"--ff-only\"], cwd=repo_root)\n                if pull_result.returncode != 0:\n                    divergence = git_upstream_divergence(repo_root)\n                    if divergence is not None:\n                        upstream, ahead, behind = divergence\n                        if ahead > 0 and behind > 0:\n                            detail = _diverged_checkout_detail(repo_root, upstream)\n                            raise CharnessError(\n                                \"managed checkout \"\n                                f\"`{repo_root}` diverged from `{upstream}` (ahead {ahead}, behind {behind}); \"\n                                \"`charness update` only fast-forwards managed checkouts. \"\n                                \"If the local commit is intentional dogfood, run \"\n                                \"`charness update --repo-root . --no-pull --skip-cli-install` from that checkout. \"\n                                f\"Otherwise rebase or reset the managed checkout onto `{upstream}` and retry.\\n\"\n                                f\"{detail}\\n\"\n                                f\"STDOUT:\\n{pull_result.stdout}\\nSTDERR:\\n{pull_result.stderr}\"\n                            )\n                expect_success(pull_result, f\"git pull in `{repo_root}`\")\n                pulled = True\n            return {\n                \"repo_root\": str(repo_root),\n                \"managed\": managed,\n                \"cloned\": cloned,\n                \"pulled\": pulled,\n            }\n\n        if repo_root.exists():\n            if any(repo_root.iterdir()):\n                raise CharnessError(\n                    f\"`{repo_root}` exists but is not a charness source checkout; pass `--repo-root` or clean that path first\"\n                )\n        elif not allow_clone:\n            raise CharnessError(f\"missing source checkout `{repo_root}`\")\n\n        if not managed and not allow_clone:\n            raise CharnessError(f\"missing explicit source checkout `{repo_root}`\")\n\n `{repo_root}`\")\n        return {\"repo_root\": str(repo_root), \"managed\": managed, \"cloned\": True, \"pulled\": False}\n\n    def expect_success(result: subprocess.CompletedProcess[str], context: str) -> None:\n        if result.returncode != 0:\n            raise CharnessError(\n                f\"{context} failed with exit code {result.returncode}\\nSTDOUT:\\n{result.stdout}\\nSTDERR:\\n{result.stderr}\"\n            )\n\n    def extract_version(text: object) -> str | None:\n        if not isinstance(text, str):\n            return None\n        match = re.search(r\"(?<!\\d)(\\d+(?:\\.\\d+){1,}(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?)(?![0-9A-Za-z.-])\", text)\n        return match.group(1) if match else None\n\n    def fixture_release(repo: any(line.strip() for line in result.stdout.splitlines())\n\n    def git_head(path: Path) -> str | None:\n        if not is_git_checkout(path):\n            return None\n        result = run([\"git\", git_upstream_divergence(path: Path) -> tuple[str, int, int] | None:\n        upstream = git_upstream_ref(path)\n        if upstream is None:\n            return None\n        result = run([\"git\", \"rev-list\", \"--left-right\", \"--count\", f\"HEAD...{upstream}\"], cwd=path)\n        if result.returncode != 0:\n            return None\n        counts = result.stdout.strip().split()\n        if len(counts) != 2:\n            return None\n        try:\n            ahead = int(counts[0])\n            behind = int(counts[1])\n        except ValueError:\n            return None\n        return upstream, ahead, behind\n\n    def git_upstream_ref(path: Path) -> str | None:\n        result = run(\n            [\"git\", \"rev-parse\", \"--abbrev-ref\", \"--symbolic-full-name\", \".git\").exists()\n\n    def latest_release_cache_is_fresh(\n        latest_release: dict[str, object], current_version: str | None\n    ) -> bool:\n        checked_at = maybe_reexec_refreshed_cli(\n        checkout_repo_root: Path,\n        *,\n        running_cli: Path | None = None,\n        execve: Callable[..., object] | None = None,\n    ) -> dict[str, object] | None:\n        checkout_cli = checkout_repo_root / \"charness\"\n        # Post-#873 this module is not the entrypoint: a bare `Path(__file__)`\n        # names the feature module, which never byte-matches the checkout CLI, so\n        # an omitted `running_cli` re-exec'd every run. Prefer the entry context\n        # the root shim publishes; `__file__` only serves direct unit use.\n        current_cli = (running_cli or SCRIPT_PATH or Path(__file__)).resolve()\n        reexec_child = os.environ.get(_CLI_REEXEC_GUARD_ENV) == str(os.getpid())\n        try:\n            if (\n                not checkout_cli.is_file()\n                or checkout_cli.resolve() == current_cli\n                or checkout_cli.read_bytes() == current_cli.read_bytes()\n            ):\n                if reexec_child:\n                    return {\"status\": \"reexecuted\", \"checkout_cli\": str(checkout_cli)}\n                return None\n        except OSError:\n            return None\n        if reexec_child:\n            emit_progress(\n                \"WARNING: running CLI still differs from the refreshed checkout after one re-exec; \"\n                \"continuing without another re-exec — if this run fails, re-run the command.\",\n            )\n            return {\n                \"status\": \"skipped\",\n                \"reason\": \"already re-executed once; loop guard active\",\n                \"checkout_cli\": str(checkout_cli),\n            }\n        # Coverage note: the lines below run but cannot be measured. A\n        # successful execve replaces the image without running atexit, so the\n        # parent's coverage shard is lost (slice E4 proves the behavior\n        # through the re-executed child's observable effects instead).\n        emit_progress(  # pragma: no cover - executes, unmeasurable; see above.\n            \"STEP: source checkout code differs from the running CLI; re-executing the str(os.getpid())  # pragma: no cover - executes, unmeasurable; see above.\n        try:  # pragma: no cover - executes, unmeasurable; see above.\n            (execve or os.execve)(  # pragma: no cover unreachable in practice: the kernel only refuses what the pre-flight stat/read already validated.\n            emit_progress(\n                f\"WARNING: re-exec of the refreshed CLI failed ({exc}); continuing with the running CLI — \"\n                \"if this run fails, re-run the command.\",\n            )\n            return {\n                \"status\": \"failed\",\n                \"reason\": f\"re-exec failed: {exc}\",\n                \"checkout_cli\": str(checkout_cli),\n            }\n        return None\n\n    def normalize_release_payload(repo: str, payload: dict[str, object]) -> dict[str, object]:\n        latest_tag = payload.get(\"tag_name\")\n        html_url = payload.get(\"html_url\")\n        published_at = payload.get(\"published_at\")\n        assets = payload.get(\"assets\", [])\n        asset_names = [\n            asset[\"name\"]\n            for asset in assets\n            if isinstance(asset, dict) and isinstance(asset.get(\"name\"), str)\n        ]\n        release = {\n            \"provider\": \"github\",\n            \"repo\": repo,\n            \"status\": \"ok\",\n            \"api_url\": str) else None,\n            \"latest_version\": extract_version(latest_tag),\n            \"published_at\": published_at if isinstance(published_at, str) else None,\n            \"asset_names\": asset_names,\n            \"error\": None,\n        }\n        if isinstance(payload.get(\"status\"), str):\n            release[\"status\"] = payload[\"status\"]\n        if isinstance(payload.get(\"error\"), str):\n            release[\"error\"] = payload[\"error\"]\n        return release\n\n    def normalize_version_token(value: object) -> str | None:\n        if not \"Z\")\n\n    def packaging_version(path: Path) -> str | None:\n        manifest_path = path / \"packaging\" / f\"{PACKAGE_ID}.json\"\n        if not manifest_path.is_file():\n            return parse_iso_timestamp(value: object) -> datetime | None:\n        if not isinstance(value, str) or not value:\n            return None\n        normalized = value.replace(\"Z\", \"+00:00\")\n        try:\n            return datetime.fromisoformat(normalized)\n        except ValueError:\n            return None\n\n    def parse_semver_like(\n        value: object,\n    ) -> tuple[tuple[int, ...], tuple[tuple[int, int | str], ...]] | None:\n        normalized = normalize_version_token(value)\n        if normalized is None:\n            return None\n        core, _, prerelease = normalized.partition(\"-\")\n        try:\n            core_parts = tuple(int(part) for part in core.split(\".\"))\n        except ValueError:\n            return None\n        if not core_parts:\n tuple(_parse_prerelease_identifier(part) for part in prerelease.split(\".\") if part)\n            if prerelease\n            else ()\n        )\n        return core_parts, prerelease_parts\n\n    def probe_self_release(repo: str | None = None) -> dict[str, object]:\n        # Imported here, not at module scope: `urllib.request` pulls in `http.client`,\n        # `ssl`, and `email.parser`, which measured 17ms of this CLI's ~114ms startup —\n        # paid by every `charness` invocation, while the one network path that needs it\n        # is this function, past the fixture short-circuit.\n normalize_release_payload(repo, fixture)\n\n        request = urllib.request.Request(\n            f\"https://api.github.com/repos/{repo}/releases/latest\",\n            headers={\n                \"Accept\": \"application/vnd.github+json\",\n                \"User-Agent\": \"charness-self-update-check\",\n            },\n        )\n        try:\n            with urllib.request.urlopen(request, timeout=10) as response:\n                payload = json.loads(response.read().decode(\"utf-8\"))  # pragma: no cover - needs a live registry response body; offline runs use the fixture short-circuit or the error branches below.\n        except urllib.error.HTTPError as exc:\n            status = \"no-release\" if exc.code == 404 else \"error\"\n            return {\n                \"provider\": \"github\",\n                \"repo\": repo,\n                \"status\": status,\n                \"api_url\": f\"https://api.github.com/repos/{repo}/releases/latest\",\n                \"html_url\": None,\n                \"latest_tag\": None,\n                \"latest_version\": None,\n                \"published_at\": None,\n                \"asset_names\": [],\n                \"error\": f\"http {exc.code}\",\n            }\n        except (TimeoutError, urllib.error.URLError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:\n            return {\n                \"provider\": \"github\",\n                \"repo\": repo,\n                \"status\": \"error\",\n                \"api_url\": f\"https://api.github.com/repos/{repo}/releases/latest\",\n                \"html_url\": None,\n                \"latest_tag\": None,\n                \"latest_version\": None,\n                \"published_at\": None,\n                \"asset_names\": [],\n                \"error\": str(exc),\n            }\n        return normalize_release_payload(repo, payload)  # pragma: no cover - live-response-only; see above.\n\n    def read_install_state(home_root: Path) -> tuple[Path, bool] | None:\n        path = default_install_state_path(home_root)\n        if not path.is_file():\n            return None\n        try:\n            payload = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n        except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):\n            return None\n        repo_root = payload.get(\"repo_root\")\n        managed_checkout = payload.get(\"managed_checkout\")\n        if not isinstance(repo_root, str) or not isinstance(managed_checkout, bool):\n            return None\n        return Path(repo_root).resolve(), managed_checkout\n\n    def read_version_state(home_root: Path) -> dict[str, object]:\n        path = default_version_state_path(home_root)\n        if not path.is_file():\n            return {\"state_version\": 1}\n        try:\n            payload = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n        except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):\n            return {\"state_version\": 1}\n        if not isinstance(payload, dict):\n            return {\"state_version\": 1}\n        payload.setdefault(\"state_version\", 1)\n        return payload\n\n    def refresh_self_release_state(\n        *,\n        home_root: Path,\n        current_version: str | None,\n    ) -> dict[str, object]:\n        latest_release = probe_self_release()\n        latest_release[\"checked_at\"] = now_iso()\n        latest_release[\"current_version\"] = current_version\n        latest_release[\"update_available\"] = compute_update_available(\n            current_version, latest_release.get(\"latest_version\")\n        )\n        return write_version_state(home_root, latest_release=latest_release)\n\n    def render_yaml(payload: object) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Render one portable YAML document for the public CLI boundary.\"\"\"\n        normalized = json.loads(json.dumps(payload, ensure_ascii=False, allow_nan=False))\n        try:\n            import yaml\n        except ImportError:\n            # JSON is valid YAML. \":\")) + \"\\n\"\n        return yaml.safe_dump(normalized, allow_unicode=True, sort_keys=False)\n\n    def resolve_repo_root(home_root: Path, explicit_repo_root: Path | None) -> tuple[Path, bool]:\n        if explicit_repo_root is not None:\n            return explicit_repo_root.resolve(), False\n        remembered = read_install_state(home_root)\n        if remembered is not None and remembered[1] is True:\n            return remembered\n        return managed_checkout_root(home_root), True\n\n    def resolve_runtime_paths(args: argparse.Namespace) -> tuple[Path, Path, Path, Path]:\n        home_root = args.home_root.resolve()\n        plugin_root = (\n            args.plugin_root.resolve() if args.plugin_root else default_plugin_root(home_root)\n        )\n        codex_marketplace_path = (\n            args.codex_marketplace_path.resolve()\n            if args.codex_marketplace_path\n            else default_codex_marketplace_path(home_root)\n        )\n = args.cli_path.resolve() if args.cli_path else default_cli_path(home_root)\n        return plugin_root, codex_marketplace_path, claude_wrapper_path, cli_path\n\n    def resolve_state_home(home_root: Path) -> Path:\n        override = os.environ.get(\"CHARNESS_STATE_HOME\")\n        if override:\n            return Path(override).expanduser().resolve()\n        xdg_root = os.environ.get(\"XDG_STATE_HOME\")\n        if xdg_root:\n            return Path(xdg_root).expanduser().resolve()\n        return home_root / \".local\" / \"state\"\n\n    def resolve_target_repo_root(target_repo_root: Path | None) -> Path:\n        return target_repo_root.resolve() if target_repo_root is not None else Path.cwd().resolve()\n\n    def resolve_tool_repo_root(args: argparse.Namespace) -> tuple[Path, bool]:\n        home_root = args.home_root.resolve()\n        repo_root, managed_checkout = resolve_repo_root(home_root, args.repo_root)\n        ensure_checkout(\n            repo_root,\n            managed=managed_checkout,\n            repo_url=getattr(args, \"repo_url\", REPO_URL),\n            allow_clone=False,\n            allow_pull=False,\n        )\n        return repo_root, managed_checkout\n\n    def run(\n        command: list[str], *, cwd: Path | None = None, env: dict[str, str] | None = None\n    ) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:\n        from scripts.core.subprocess_guard import run_process\n\n        # The repo's one spawn primitive (#768). No timeout so callers keep the\n        # historical wait-forever contract; `None` cwd still inherits.\n        return run_process(command, cwd=cwd or Path.cwd(), env=env, timeout_seconds=None)\n    def self_release_repo() -> str:\n        match = re.search(r\"github\\.com/([^/]+/[^/]+?)(?:\\.git)?$\", REPO_URL)\n        return match.group(1) if match else \"corca-ai/charness\"\n\n    def should_auto_refresh_self_release(\n        args: argparse.Namespace, provenance: dict[str, object]\n    ) -> bool:\n        if provenance.get(\"eligible_for_auto_update_check\") is not True:\n            return False\n        if bool_env(\"CHARNESS_NO_UPDATE_CHECK\"):\n            return False\n        if bool_env(\"CI\"):\n            return False\n        if getattr(args, \"command\", None) == \"version\" and getattr(args, \"check\", False):\n            return False\n        if bool_env(\"CHARNESS_FORCE_UPDATE_CHECK\"):\n            return True\n        return sys.stdout.isatty() and sys.stderr.isatty()\n\n    def write_version_state(\n        home_root: Path,\n        *,\n        provenance: dict[str, object] | None = None,\n        latest_release: dict[str, object] | None = None,\n    ) -> dict[str, object]:\n        path = default_version_state_path(home_root)\n        state = read_version_state(home_root)\n        if provenance is not None:\n            state[\"version_provenance\"] = provenance\n        if latest_release is not None:\n            state[\"latest_release\"] = latest_release\n        try:\n            path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            path.write_text(\n                json.dumps(state, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2) + \"\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\"\n            )\n        except OSError:\n            # Version probes must stay usable even when host-local state is read-only.\n            return state\n        return state\n\n\ndef _load_feature(module_name: str, func_name: str, args=None, check=None):\n    \"\"\"Import a command payload from the target tree, setting entry context.\"\"\"\n    # Defaults to a dry-run preview.\",\n    )\n    tool_repair_parser.set_defaults(func=cmd_tool_repair)\n\n    tool_sync_support_parser = tool_subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"sync-support\",\n        help=\"Refresh cache-backed support skill materialization for one or more external tools.\",\n    )\n    add_common_tool_args(tool_sync_support_parser)\n    add_support_sync_args(tool_sync_support_parser)\n    tool_sync_support_parser.add_argument(\"--dry-run\", action=\"store_true\")\n    tool_sync_support_parser.set_defaults(func=cmd_tool_sync_support)\n\n    tool_install_parser = tool_subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"install\",\n        help=\"Attempt tool installation where the manifest allows it, otherwise persist install guidance and doctor state.\",\n    )\n    add_common_tool_args(tool_install_parser)\n    add_support_sync_args(tool_install_parser)\n    tool_install_parser.add_argument(\"--dry-run\", action=\"store_true\")\n    tool_install_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--skip-sync-support\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Skip support skill rematerialization after install guidance or execution.\",\n    )\n    tool_install_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--recommend-for-skill\",\n        help=\"Install tools declared as supporting a public skill instead of passing explicit tool ids.\",\n    )\n    tool_install_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--recommendation-role\",\n        choices=(\"runtime\", \"validation\"),\n        help=\"Install tools with a recommendation role, optionally scoped by --next-skill-id.\",\n    )\n    tool_install_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--next-skill-id\",\n        default=\"quality\",\n        help=\"Public skill id used with --recommendation-role; defaults to quality.\",\n    )\n    tool_install_parser.set_defaults(func=cmd_tool_install)\n\n    tool_update_parser = tool_subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"update\",\n        help=\"Attempt manifest-declared external tool updates, then refresh support skill materialization and doctor state.\",\n    )\n    add_common_tool_args(tool_update_parser)\n    add_support_sync_args(tool_update_parser)\n    tool_update_parser.add_argument(\"--dry-run\", action=\"store_true\")\n    tool_update_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--skip-sync-support\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Skip support skill rematerialization after update.\",\n    )\n    tool_update_parser.set_defaults(func=cmd_tool_update)\n\n    worktree_parser = subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"worktree\",\n        help=\"Create, inspect, prepare, and clean up git worktrees so mutate-phase work runs against installed dependencies and live hooks.\",\n    )\n    worktree_subparsers = worktree_parser.add_subparsers(dest=\"worktree_command\", required=True)\n\n    def add_worktree_lib_args(command: argparse.ArgumentParser) -> None:\n        command.add_argument(\n            \"--home-root\",\n            type=Path,\n            default=default_home_root(),\n            help=\"Home root used to locate the managed charness checkout when the entrypoint is a PATH shim.\",\n        )\n        command.add_argument(\n            \"--charness-checkout\",\n            type=Path,\n            help=\"Explicit charness source checkout to load worktree helpers from. Defaults to the embedded or managed checkout.\",\n        )\n\n    def add_worktree_create_parser(command_name: str, *, help_text: str) -> None:\n        command = worktree_subparsers.add_parser(command_name, help=help_text)\n        command.add_argument(\n            \"--repo-root\",\n            type=Path,\n            help=\"Repo root that the worktree should be created under. Defaults to the current working directory.\",\n        )\n        command.add_argument(\n            \"--path\", type=Path, required=True, help=\"Path for the new git worktree.\"\n        )\n        command.add_argument(\"--branch\", help=\"Create a new local branch for the worktree.\")\n        command.add_argument(\"--base\", help=\"Base ref passed to `git worktree add` after the path.\")\n        command.add_argument(\n            \"--detach\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"Create a detached-HEAD worktree.\"\n        )\n        command.add_argument(\n            \"--prepare\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"Run readiness prepare after creation.\"\n        )\n        command.add_argument(\n            \"--dry-run\",\n            action=\"store_true\",\n            help=\"Print the planned git command without creating the worktree.\",\n        )\n        command.add_argument(\n            \"--force\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"Pass --force to `git worktree add`.\"\n        )\n        lifetime = command.add_mutually_exclusive_group()\n        lifetime.add_argument(\n            \"--ephemeral\",\n            action=\"store_true\",\n            help=\"Mark the worktree disposable: create reclaims expired leftovers and caps residue.\",\n        )\n        lifetime.add_argument(\n            \"--owned\",\n            action=\"store_true\",\n            help=\"Keep the worktree until `charness worktree cleanup`; never auto-removed.\",\n        )\n        add_worktree_lib_args(command)\n        command.set_defaults(func=cmd_worktree_create)\n\n    add_worktree_create_parser(\n        \"create\",\n        help_text=\"Create a git worktree, then run readiness doctor and optional prepare.\",\n    )\n    add_worktree_create_parser(\n        \"add\",\n        help_text=\"Alias for `create`: wrap `git worktree add` with readiness doctor and optional prepare.\",\n    )\n\n    worktree_exec_parser = worktree_subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"exec\",\n        help=\"Run one command in an isolated worktree with external runtime caches.\",\n        description=\"Run one command in an isolated worktree with external runtime caches.\",\n    )\n    worktree_exec_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--repo-root\",\n        type=Path,\n        help=\"Worktree in which to run the command. Defaults to the current working directory.\",\n    )\n    worktree_exec_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--allow-main\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Allow an intentional command in the primary worktree; parent writes are then possible.\",\n    )\n    worktree_exec_parser.add_argument(\n        \"command\", nargs=argparse.REMAINDER, help=\"Command after `--`.\"\n    )\n    add_worktree_lib_args(worktree_exec_parser)\n    worktree_exec_parser.set_defaults(func=cmd_worktree_exec)\n\n    worktree_doctor_parser = worktree_subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"doctor\",\n        help=\"Probe worktree readiness (isolation, hooksPath, lefthook shim resolution, husky directory, manifest checks).\",\n    )\n    worktree_doctor_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--repo-root\",\n        type=Path,\n        help=\"Worktree to inspect. Defaults to the current working directory.\",\n    )\n    worktree_doctor_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--require-isolation\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=(\n            \"Fail unless this checkout is a linked worktree rather than the main one. Pass it \"\n            \"before handing a WRITE-CAPABLE agent a checkout: without isolation that agent \"\n            \"shares the parent's tree and index, and a stray git op lands in the parent's \"\n            \"commit. Without the flag, isolation is reported as a fact and never enforced.\"\n        ),\n    )\n    add_worktree_lib_args(worktree_doctor_parser)\n    worktree_doctor_parser.set_defaults(func=cmd_worktree_doctor)\n\n    worktree_prepare_parser = worktree_subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"prepare\",\n        help=\"Run the worktree adapter's prepare commands and re-validate readiness.\",\n    )\n    worktree_prepare_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--repo-root\",\n        type=Path,\n        help=\"Worktree to prepare. Defaults to the current working directory.\",\n    )\n    worktree_prepare_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--force\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Run prepare even if doctor already reports pass.\",\n    )\n    worktree_prepare_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--no-dependency-reuse\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Do not link an installed dependency tree; the declared install command runs unless doctor coverage skips prepare (add --force to override that).\",\n    )\n    add_worktree_lib_args(worktree_prepare_parser)\n    worktree_prepare_parser.set_defaults(func=cmd_worktree_prepare)\n\n    worktree_audit_parser = worktree_subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"audit\",\n        help=\"Survey all worktrees registered to the repository and classify primary/active/prunable/stale.\",\n    )\n    worktree_audit_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--repo-root\",\n        type=Path,\n        help=\"Repo root whose worktree registry should be audited. Defaults to the current working directory.\",\n    )\n    worktree_audit_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--stale-days\",\n        type=int,\n        default=14,\n        help=\"Detached-HEAD worktrees older than this many days are reported as stale (default: 14).\",\n    )\n    worktree_audit_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--prune\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"After audit, reclaim expired ephemeral worktrees and prune metadata for missing ones.\",\n    )\n    worktree_audit_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--doctor\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Run readiness doctor for existing worktrees and include per-worktree readiness summaries.\",\n    )\n    add_worktree_lib_args(worktree_audit_parser)\n    worktree_audit_parser.set_defaults(func=cmd_worktree_audit)\n\n    worktree_cleanup_parser = worktree_subparsers.add_parser(\n        \"cleanup\",\n        help=\"Safely remove a registered git worktree and optionally delete its merged local branch.\",\n    )\n    worktree_cleanup_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--repo-root\",\n        type=Path,\n        help=\"Repo root whose worktree should be operated on. Defaults to the current working directory.\",\n    )\n    worktree_cleanup_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--path\", type=Path, required=True, help=\"Registered worktree path to remove.\"\n    )\n    worktree_cleanup_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--delete-merged-branch\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Delete the local branch only after it is contained in --branch-base.\",\n    )\n    worktree_cleanup_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--branch-base\",\n        default=\"HEAD\",\n        help=\"Local ref that must contain the target branch before branch deletion; defaults to HEAD.\",\n    )\n    worktree_cleanup_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--yes\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Execute the planned cleanup. Defaults to dry-run.\",\n    )\n    worktree_cleanup_parser.add_argument(\n        \"--force\",\n        action=\"store_true\",\n        help=\"Pass --force to git worktree remove for dirty targets.\",\n    )\n    add_worktree_lib_args(worktree_cleanup_parser)\n    worktree_cleanup_parser.set_defaults(func=cmd_worktree_cleanup)\n\n    return parser\n\n\ndef main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:\n    raw_argv = list(sys.argv[1:] if argv is None else argv)\n    if raw_argv == [\"--version\"]:\n        raw_argv = [\"version\"]\n    parser = build_parser()\n    args = parser.parse_args(raw_argv)\n    is_worktree_exec = (\n        getattr(args, \"command\", None) == \"worktree\"\n        and getattr(args, \"worktree_command\", None) == \"exec\"\n    )\n    is_task_run = (\n        getattr(args, \"command\", None) == \"task\" and getattr(args, \"task_command\", None) == \"run\"\n    )\n    runtime_configured = False\n    if not (is_worktree_exec or is_task_run):\n        runtime_configured = _configure_runtime_for_repo(\n            _runtime_repo_for_args(args), required=False\n        )\n    if not (\n        getattr(args, \"command\", None) == \"version\"\n        and not getattr(args, \"verbose\", False)\n        and not getattr(args, \"check\", False)\n    ) and not (is_worktree_exec or is_task_run):\n        maybe_record_self_version_state(args)\n    if _EARLY_BYTECODE_GUARD and runtime_configured:\n        sys.dont_write_bytecode = False\n    try:\n        return args.func(args)\n    finally:\n        if _EARLY_BYTECODE_GUARD:\n            sys.dont_write_bytecode = False\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    try:\n        raise SystemExit(main())\n    except CharnessError as exc:\n        print(str(exc), file=sys.stderr)\n        raise SystemExit(1)\n",

  {
    "carrier_path": "charness-artifacts/critique/workers/release-8150-final-followup25/semantic-input/0001-content.bin",
    "carrier_sha256": "2e4c19b79cbfc574ecd85d01e0cb742b67444a56dc4c0a8f239718b7160612c7",
    "content_sha256": "08a58d3db3a87be72af31cdd162bf1a83a934534d0fd75d94fa05e19db484ae9",
    "disposition": "present",
    "path": "charness-artifacts/release/2026-10-20-v8.15.0-notes.md",
    "prompt_content": "# Charness v8.15.0\n\n## What changed\n\n- `charness task run` no longer stops a lane on a wall clock by default. A progress rule bounds every lane instead. A window of `CHARNESS_TASK_RUN_PROGRESS_WINDOW_SECONDS` (default `1200` seconds; `0` turns the rule off, and a negative value falls back to the default) can pass with no new commit, no file change, and no new command result. When that happens, charness interrupts the executor and delivers a checkpoint request the way it delivers a steer message, by resuming the executor's session where it can: commit what is coherent and report, reply `BLOCKED:`, or keep going. If the next window also shows no progress, charness stops the lane itself (`NO-PROGRESS-STOP:`). A command that started since the previous check and is still running at a check defers the request, and so the stop, by one more check. Windows are nominal: each check runs at the watch's next poll, so it can run late. No human or orchestrator has to watch for the stop to happen. A test, lint, or typecheck command rerun with the same text on an unchanged tree is not progress. When charness can read the worktree, the lane is asked on such a command's 3rd run to switch to the debug skill, on its 4th to checkpoint, and on its 5th the lane is stopped. The window check never kills a command younger than one window, but a loop message or stop does not wait for a running command. A command still running a window later can be killed, so set the window above the longest command a lane legitimately runs. Codex resume after this kill was probed live. Muse resume after a kill was not probed. `--timeout-seconds` still works as an explicit hard cap, but it no longer has a default. For the final attempt, `progress_guard.progress_rule` in the task result records each window check, rule message, and rule stop, and `execution.preemptions` counts preemptions when there were some. `task status` shows the live window state.\n- Executor lanes that `charness task run` launches now have network access inside their workspace-write sandbox. Codex lanes get `-c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true` and Muse lanes get `--sandbox-network enabled`. Before this, the lane sandbox refused tests that bind a local port or fetch through the proxy, with `listen EPERM` or a proxy `502` response. The release tests check the flags the carrier passes, not a bind or fetch inside a sandbox. The read-only reviewers (self-review and the brief critique) drop that setting, so their sandbox is unchanged.\n- `charness task run` now stamps each JSON-object line of a lane's `<executor>.events.log` with `charness_received_at`, the UTC time the carrier received that line from the executor's pipe. Before this, Codex items and Muse envelopes carried no wall time, so a lane could not say how long a command or phase took. Non-JSON lines are unchanged, and so are the rest of each event's bytes.\n- `charness task status <task-id> --commands` reads that stamped log back and reports the lane's longest commands, how many commands it started in each family (test, lint, typecheck, merge, matched by command patterns, so the counts are approximate), and its time per phase. The option is refused without a task id. Durations are receive-time estimates. Pipe buffering can delay either end of a command, so a duration can differ from the executor's own timing in either direction. A stamp without a timezone counts as unstamped. A lane run before this release has an unstamped events log, so for that lane the report counts command starts but gives no durations or phase times.\n- Stamping never loses event bytes silently between the executor's pipe and the lane log. Unless one of the loss cases below occurs, every byte the executor writes before its pipe closes reaches the lane's stdout log, on normal exit, timeout, interrupt, and preemption, and a Codex lane then appends that log unchanged to `codex.events.log`. Charness adds only the stamp field on JSON lines and a newline after an unterminated final line. The loss cases are a descendant process that keeps the pipe open past the process-group kill, an event reader held past the close grace by the progress rule's worktree probes (recorded as an open writer or a reader error), a pipe read that fails, a log write that fails or accepts only part of a line, and a log write that hangs, which stops the pipe from draining and leaves the rest uncopied. Each is recorded in the task result as `execution.event_capture_incomplete`. After a failed or short write the reader keeps draining the pipe, so the executor does not block on a full pipe. A Muse lane copies only a bounded tail of its stdout log into `muse.events.log` and skips an invocation that emitted no Muse event; that copy is outside this guarantee.\n- The debug skill names a follow-up record after the branch that writes it. Before this, every follow-up was named by counting the files in its own worktree, so parallel lanes picked the same number and their merges collided. When the skill can read the checked-out branch from the git files and it is not `main`, `master`, `trunk`, or `develop`, every follow-up record's name, including the first, ends in `-followup-<slug>-<hash>.md` (with the default title, `<date>-debug-review-followup-<slug>-<hash>.md`), and a further follow-up from the same branch on the same day adds a `-2` suffix. `<slug>` is the lane id for a `task/<lane>` branch and the branch name otherwise, as a lowercase slug (`branch` when nothing survives), and `<hash>` is a short hash of the full branch name. Records from different branches of that kind differ unless their hashes share a prefix. On a default branch, a detached HEAD, or branch metadata the skill cannot read, the old counter remains, so worktrees there can still pick the same name. The `latest.md` pointer works as before, so parallel lanes that each write a follow-up can still both change `latest.md`.\n\n## Update\n\nRun `charness update`, then run `charness version` to confirm the installed version. No migration is required. A launcher that passes `--timeout-seconds` keeps that hard cap until it drops the flag; the cap applies per executor invocation, and each preemption or resume starts a new one. To rely on the progress rule alone, drop the flag. Set `CHARNESS_TASK_RUN_PROGRESS_WINDOW_SECONDS` above the longest command your lanes legitimately run if that exceeds the default. `--no-progress-seconds` is still the separate first-edit budget for require-change lanes, not the progress window. Lanes without `--require-change` can now end with `NO-PROGRESS-STOP:` too. A window of `0` with no `--timeout-seconds` leaves a lane with no bound. Lanes now have network access, and this release has no per-lane switch to turn it off.\n\n## Derived claim surfaces\n\n<!-- charness:derived-claims:begin -->\n<!-- Generated from the shipped tree. Hand-edits are refused at publish: the gate re-derives this block and compares. -->\n\n<!-- claim-surface: json-declaring-scripts -->\n\n```yaml\nid: json-declaring-scripts\nquestion: which repo-owned Python sources declare their own `--json` option\ncount: 0\nitems: []\nscanned:\n- scripts/**/*.py\n- skills/**/scripts/**/*.py\n- charness\nunscanned:\n- the exported `plugins/charness/**` mirror and `mutants/**`, which are generated\n  copies of these same sources\n- flag names composed at runtime from a variable rather than written as a string literal\n- flags a wrapper forwards without declaring, which no parser in this tree names\n```\n\n<!-- claim-surface: charness-subcommands -->\n\n```yaml\nid: charness-subcommands\nquestion: which top-level subcommands the `charness` CLI declares\ncount: 14\nitems:\n- capability\n- catalog\n- doctor\n- goal\n- hooks\n- init\n- reset\n- task\n- tool\n- train\n- uninstall\n- update\n- version\n- worktree\nscanned:\n- charness\nunscanned:\n- nested subcommand groups such as `charness task run`, which are declared on their\n  own subparser\n- subcommands a host adapter adds outside this entrypoint\n```\n\n<!-- claim-surface: public-skills -->\n\n```yaml\nid: public-skills\nquestion: which public skills this repo ships\ncount: 19\nitems:\n- achieve\n- announcement\n- create-cli\n- create-skill\n- critique\n- debug\n- gather\n- hitl\n- hotl\n- ideation\n- impl\n- issue\n- narrative\n- prove\n- quality\n- release\n- retro\n- setup\n- spec\nscanned:\n- skills/public/*/SKILL.md\nunscanned:\n- support, profile, and integration skills, which are not part of the public surface\n- skills a consuming repo installs from elsewhere\n```\n\n<!-- claim-surface: repo-shell-gates -->\n\n```yaml\nid: repo-shell-gates\nquestion: which `check-*.sh` shell gates this repo ships\ncount: 8\nitems:\n- check-docs.sh\n- check-links-external.sh\n- check-links-internal.sh\n- check-markdown.sh\n- check-python-lint.sh\n- check-rust.sh\n- check-secrets.sh\n- check-shell.sh\nscanned:\n- scripts/**/check-*.sh\nunscanned:\n- Python gates under `scripts/`, which are far more numerous and are not this surface\n- gates a consuming repo wires from its own tree\n```\n\n<!-- charness:derived-claims:end -->\n",

    "carrier_path": "charness-artifacts/critique/workers/release-8150-final-followup25/semantic-input/0002-content.bin",
    "carrier_sha256": "369a78d33c58fdc585c09cf9e6e99b239b2ecf369ed7580e30c5673dc9e0ef53",
    "content_sha256": "a5225854294d788e00850ffbf12f9a4f6e637a5245b9e97441f4b3f948a152cf",

    "path": "charness-artifacts/spec/lane-progress-rule.md",
    "prompt_content": "# Lane progress rule: replace the wall-clock lane timeout\n\nStatus: current (2026-10-20). Revised after a two-reviewer design premortem; dispositions at the end.\n\n## Source (verbatim)\n\nOperator, 2026-10-20 18:5x KST: \"한쪽은 시간이 짧아서 문제, 다른 쪽은 길어서 문제네. 이 시간 제한 자체를 없애고, 대신 시간 제한으로 이루려고 했던 걸 더 효율적/효과적으로 할 더 좋은 방법이 없나?\"\n\nOperator, 2026-10-20 19:0x KST, on a proposed separate generous backstop timer: \"'넉넉한 안전판'을 따로 둘 필요가 있나? 어차피 계속 볼텐데? 레스 벗 베터로 할 수는 없나?\" The primary's restatement: drop the separate backstop; keep a single no-progress rule. \"No progress\" means no new commit, no file change and no new command outcome since the last check. Its terminal action is charness itself stopping the lane after a checkpoint request goes unanswered. A dead or silent executor is the extreme case of the same rule, so there is no second timer. The stop must not depend on a human or the primary watching. Delete the wall-clock default.\n\nEvidence from the consuming repo (ceal, 2026-10-20, reported by its primary, not re-verified here): lanes run with `--timeout-seconds 5400`; 24 of about 133 lanes timed out; one sweep timed out four times in a row, the last while fixing pre-commit findings just before its commit. Each timeout leaves a `task-run: WIP candidate — interrupted mid-edit — state unknown` commit that cannot be merged and must be continued. One lane ran its focused tests 39 times, another 56 times.\n\n## What the timeout was for\n\n(a) stop a lane that loops without progress; (b) free capacity; (c) give the orchestrator a judgment point. A wall clock does all three badly: it kills productive lanes mid-edit and lets a looping lane burn its whole budget.\n\n## Current owners reused (baseline before this change)\n\n- Before this change, `charness task run --timeout-seconds` (default 3600) was the only wall clock; `run_monitored_phase` kills the executor at it and the carrier commits a WIP candidate.\n- `LaneProgressWatch` (`scripts/task_run/task_run_progress.py`) already polls lane logs and the worktree every 15 s beside a require-change lane, owns the kill of the executor's process group, records `NO-PROGRESS-STOP:` and `progress_stopped`, and enforces two narrower guards: the first-edit budget (CONTRACT-READ to first EDITING or scoped diff, `--no-progress-seconds`, default 300 s) and the BLOCKED linger grace (60 s).\n- The steer queue (`charness task steer <id> --message`, `steer.queue.jsonl`) delivers typed messages only at an executor turn boundary: the carrier resumes the same session (`codex exec resume <thread>` / Muse `--session-id`) with the messages appended. A Codex lane is usually one long turn, so today a message reaches it only after it finishes.\n- v8.15.0 stamps every executor event line as it arrives (`StampingPipe`), and `task_run_event_timing` already parses Codex `command_execution` and Muse `tool.bash` start/completion into commands and families (test, lint, typecheck).\n\n## What is missing, and the one new mechanism\n\nMid-turn delivery. The carrier gains **preemption**: to deliver its own message now, it kills the executor's process group and resumes the same session with the message, through the existing steer delivery path (`codex exec resume <thread>`, Muse `--session-id`), falling back to the existing relaunch-in-place (fresh session, same worktree, prompt plus messages) when no session id is known or the resume fails. Probe on this machine (codex-cli 0.163.0): a `codex exec --json` session SIGKILLed during a running `sleep 40` resumed with `codex exec resume <thread>`, kept its context (it quoted the first instruction), and logged only a missing tool-call output. Muse resume after a kill is not probed; its fallback is relaunch-in-place.\n\nPreemption is not free, so the rule limits where it happens:\n\n- At a command completion (loop messages), the completed command is not interrupted, but the carrier does not wait for any other command still running: an executor that runs commands in parallel can have one killed, with possible partial effects, as well as the model's in-progress reasoning.\n- At a window check, the carrier never kills a command younger than one window (one that started after the previous check). Such a command defers the request, or the stop, until it completes or the next check, whichever is first; only then are the running commands, each of which has run for at least `W`, killed with the executor's process group, and the message names every one of them as interrupted with possibly partial effects.\n- One preemption per invocation: a second message raised before the executor dies is queued and rides the same resume. Once a stop has won (in the watch or already in the rule), a later message is suppressed and counted (`suppressed_actions`), never queued; a message queued earlier in the same invocation is nacked `superseded-by-stop` and its preemption uncounted.\n- Each invocation has its own command state: a resumed Codex session reuses item ids (`item_1` again), so in-flight commands are keyed per invocation and a restarted command is young again.\n\nPreemption leaves the worktree as it is, including any partial effect of a killed command; the carrier does not checkpoint, commit, or clean it. A preempted invocation has its own result: it is not a failure, not a retry attempt (attempts live outside the invocation loop), not a relaunch in the friction log, not a failed resume, and not a WIP candidate. `execution.preemptions` counts them; relaunch friction counts only real relaunches. If the executor exits on its own just before the kill, the queued message is delivered at that turn boundary exactly like an operator steer.\n\n## The rule\n\nOne knob: the progress window `W` (env `CHARNESS_TASK_RUN_PROGRESS_WINDOW_SECONDS`, default 1200 s; `0` turns the rule off; a negative value falls back to the default). It applies to every lane run through `charness task run`, not only require-change lanes.\n\nProgress since the last check is any of:\n\n1. a new commit or file change: the worktree fingerprint (`HEAD`, the tracked diff, untracked files by size and mtime; ignored files do not count) differs from the last check;\n2. a new outcome in the executor's own events: a completed file-change item (Codex `file_change`; Muse `tool.edit`, `tool.edit_file`, or `tool.write_file`; reads, searches, and todo lists are not outcomes), or a completed command whose pair (command text, worktree fingerprint at completion) has not completed before in this lane. Rerunning the same command on an unchanged tree is not a new outcome.\n\nSignals are three-valued. An unobservable worktree contributes nothing, neither progress nor its absence: the executor's events are always observed, so a silent executor is still reclaimed, and an editing executor still shows file-change items. With the tree unreadable, a command completion is keyed to one \"unobservable\" state: a new command is still new, a rerun is not, and no loop message is sent. The watch runs the rule even when its other polls fail; if the rule itself fails 8 consecutive times (no completed check in between; quiet polls do not reset the count), the watch stops the lane with that reason rather than leave it unbounded.\n\nEvery `W` the watch checks:\n\n- progress: nothing happens. An outstanding request is recorded as answered.\n- no progress, no request outstanding: charness sends a **checkpoint request** (\"No commit, file change, or new command result in the last W. If your work has a coherent state, commit it (hooks must pass; never --no-verify) and write your final report naming what remains; if you are blocked, reply BLOCKED: <reason>. If you are still progressing, continue; charness stops this lane if the next W also shows none.\"), by preemption when no command is in flight, deferred as above otherwise.\n- no progress, request delivered and unanswered: charness **stops** the lane (`NO-PROGRESS-STOP:`, `progress_stopped`) through the existing guard kill. A request still deferred at this check is delivered now (preempting the long command) instead, and the stop waits one more window.\n\nA request is answered by any progress before the next check (`answered_by: progress`), or by the executor ending cleanly with exit 0 (`answered_by: lane-ended`; a timeout, interruption, or failure records `answered: false, answered_by: lane-failed`) (a final report or a `BLOCKED:` line ends the executor's turn; the existing BLOCKED grace covers a lane that lingers after one). A silent model or dead executor is reclaimed at the second due check after its last progress; a hung command at the third (one window of youth, one for the request, one for the stop). Checks fall due every `W` but run at the watch's next poll (15 s by default) after its previous tick returns, and a tick's own work (the rule's worktree probes, three git commands of at most 60 s each, and the live snapshot) delays the next one. Windows are therefore nominal: this spec gives no hard wall-clock bound, only the count of due checks. With `W` well above the poll and the tick's work, reclamation takes about `2W` and `3W`. No human or orchestrator needs to watch. Set `W` above the longest single command a lane legitimately runs.\n\n### Loops\n\nEvaluated at each command completion, for commands in the test, lint, or typecheck families, keyed by (exact command text, worktree fingerprint at completion):\n\n- 3rd completion of the same key (no file change between runs): preempt with \"switch to the debug skill: read the failure, state one falsifiable hypothesis, change code, test, or approach before rerunning\".\n- 4th: preempt with the checkpoint request (\"commit the coherent part, write your final report naming what is unresolved, and stop; a further identical run stops this lane\").\n- 5th: stop (the request went unanswered).\n\nExact text is deliberate: it catches the reported case (the same focused command rerun dozens of times) with no false alarm across distinct commands. A loop that varies the command text each time evades this check; it still produces outcomes, so the window does not stop it either. That gap is a recorded non-claim.\n\n## Wall-clock default\n\n`--timeout-seconds` loses its default (none: the executor runs until it ends, preempted or stopped only by the rule). The flag stays as an explicit hard cap for callers that want one; it is no longer recommended for lanes, and its stop still produces the WIP candidate. `task run --detach` stops forwarding a default. Internal reviewer and self-review carriers keep their own explicit bounds.\n\n## Receipt and live view\n\n`progress_guard.progress_rule` records the window, its source, every check (progress or not), each checkpoint or loop message, whether each request was answered, and the stop reason; `execution.preemptions` counts carrier preemptions. The `live` snapshot shows the window state (seconds since last progress, request outstanding) so `task status` readers see it without tailing logs.\n\n## Launcher contract\n\nA launcher stops passing `--timeout-seconds`. It may set `CHARNESS_TASK_RUN_PROGRESS_WINDOW_SECONDS` (per invocation or globally); otherwise the default applies. A launcher that keeps passing `--timeout-seconds 5400` keeps a 90-minute cap per executor invocation (each preemption or resume starts a new one) until it drops the flag. Nothing needs to watch the lane for the stop to happen.\n\n## Non-goals and deferred\n\n- The first-edit budget and BLOCKED grace stay as they are (they bound analysis-before-edit and a declared block, not progress).\n- No periodic checkpoint request for a lane that is progressing; it continues until it ends.\n- Relabeling the WIP commit a progress stop leaves (the tree has been static for at least W, so \"interrupted mid-edit\" overstates it) is deferred.\n- Muse resume after a SIGKILL is not probed on this machine; Muse falls back to relaunch-in-place if its resume fails.\n\n## Design critique disposition (2026-10-20)\n\nTwo file-backed read-only reviewers (`design-premortem-a`, operational; `design-premortem-b`, interface and minimality) both returned `block` on the first draft.\n\n- Act before implementing: preemption could kill a productive long command (A LP-2, B 1) — now deferred while a command is in flight and only after `W`, with partial effects named. Preemption was not distinguished from a failed resume, attempts, or relaunch friction (A LP-3, B 3) — now its own result and counter. An unobservable worktree either blocked reclamation or risked false stops (A LP-4) — now three-valued, with file-change events as an always-observed signal. The request had no defined answer (B 2) — now defined.\n- Valid but defer: loop keys by exact command text can be evaded by varying the text (A LP-1) — recorded as a non-claim; normalization risks false alarms across distinct commands.\n- Over-worry: keeping `--timeout-seconds` as an explicit opt-in; keeping the first-edit budget and BLOCKED grace (both reviewers dismissed these).\n- Outside this repository: the consuming launcher's `--timeout-seconds 5400` must be removed there.\n\n## Implementation review disposition (2026-10-20)\n\nA follow-up reviewer (`impl-followup-a`) over the first wiring returned `block`: a stop could kill a command started just after the request (LP-2), reruns on an unreadable tree counted as progress (LP-4a), a failing watch poll could leave a lane unbounded (LP-4b), preemption and stop were not serialized (LP-3), and a lane ending did not answer its request (B-2). All five are repaired as described above, each with a test.\n\nA second follow-up (`impl-followup-b`) confirmed LP-2, LP-4a and the poll half of LP-4b resolved, and blocked on three narrower points, now repaired with tests: the failure counter was reset by quiet polls between failing due checks; a message could still be queued after the rule (not yet the watch) had decided to stop, or queued and then superseded by a stop without a disposition; and a timeout or failure counted as answering the request. A live probe on this machine (real codex-cli 0.163.0 lane, 20-second window) also showed a resumed session reusing `item_1`, which hid a restarted command from the young-command rule; commands are now keyed per invocation.\n\n## Live probes (2026-10-20, this machine, codex-cli 0.163.0)\n\n- Loop: a report-only lane told to run a failing `pytest` five times was preempted after the third run with the debug request and after the fourth with the checkpoint request; both resumes kept the same thread, the lane quoted both messages and ended with its report (`preemptions: 2`, exit 0, 35 s).\n- Window: a lane told to wait on `sleep 300` with a 20 s window had the request deferred while the sleep was young, delivered (killing it) one window later, and was stopped after the request went unanswered.\n",

  {
    "carrier_path": "charness-artifacts/critique/workers/release-8150-final-followup25/semantic-input/0003-content.bin",

    "path": "docs/agent-task-runs.md",
    "prompt_content": "# Agent task runs\n\n> Status: current\n> Source of truth: this page and the `charness task run/status` implementation\n> Last verified: 2026-10-17\n\n`charness task run` executes one bounded lane; `task status` reads its external\nresult store. There is no scheduler lifecycle. `--executor` defaults to\n`codex` (`gpt-6-luna`, effort medium/xhigh/max); `muse` uses its default model\nand effort medium/high/xhigh/max.\n\nThe receipt's canonical executor block is `payload[\"executor\"]`. Codex keeps\nlegacy aliases and log names; Muse records its trusted lane worktree as the\nworkspace because its runner has one effective workspace, not Codex's\n`--add-dir` grants. Readers should branch on `executor.kind`. Executor names\nthe agent runner (`codex`/`muse`); carrier names where the candidate lives\n(`carrier_kind`: `commit-only`, `commit-plus-dirty`, `worktree-only`,\n`unknown`), not who ran it.\n\n`--require-change` shapes implementation prompts and records edit/test phases. A scope mismatch retains a typed blocker rather than silently selecting a substitute owner.\n\nThe [progress rule](../charness-artifacts/spec/lane-progress-rule.md) bounds a lane: a `CHARNESS_TASK_RUN_PROGRESS_WINDOW_SECONDS` window (default 1200) without commits, edits, or new command results delivers a checkpoint request and a later empty check stops it; a young command delays either step a check. `--timeout-seconds` is optional.\n\n`--report-only` inspects without changing and never reports a writer conflict.\nInterrupted work with a proven empty scope is `interrupted-before-edit`; WIP is\nreserved for changed work whose completeness is unknown. A useful dirty\ncandidate can be retained for review even when blockers deny approval.\n\nTerminal receipts carry `result_kind` and a stable blocker. The CLI exit-code\nmapping is owned by `charness task run --help`.\nTransient stream stalls retry in the same worktree (three attempts by default,\n30-second backoff), recording each failure kind. Finished-but-unapprovable\nwork needs review, merge, or re-scope; it does not need relaunch. Scope closure\nstays warning-only (#831), a typed scope mismatch records a\n`scope_extension_request`, and refresh excludes lane-created directories.\nSecret-pattern environment names are scrubbed except\nexecutor auth keeps and `CHARNESS_TASK_RUN_KEEP_SECRET_ENV`; the receipt records\nnames, never values.\n\n## Learning and resumed orchestration\n\nTask-run friction events use WI-1 event-schema v1 and live in\n`task-run/friction-log.jsonl` under the external task runtime root. A second\nsame-kind event from a distinct task run within 30 days carries\n`repair the pattern, don't reshape the command`; nothing repairs it\nautomatically. Writer\nconflicts are recorded for operator resolution.\n\n`charness task report [--window 30m]` renders the fixed-shape period report\nfrom lane metrics, the friction log, and the decision ledger: goals, lane\nstates, friction with repairs, measured improvements with the period\n`improvements found:` line, decisions plus what needs the operator, and the\nnext-action table. Every section carries measured numbers, not prose claims.\n\nKeep the active principles, DAG file, and handoff in the parent repo's\n`.charness/task-run/orchestration-pointers.md`; every newly built lane prompt\nreads it when nonempty, so a resumed orchestrator restores those pointers at\nits next launch (the builder does not detect host compaction).\n\n## Run\n\n```bash\ncharness task run \\\n  --repo-root . \\\n  --lane feature-lane \\\n  --scope src/example.py \\\n  --prompt \"Implement the requested slice and run its focused tests\" \\\n  --effort xhigh\n```\n\nA clean parent is required. Model/effort identity, scope expansion, candidate\ncarrier, `changed_line_gate`, and retention live in\n[`task_run_contract.py`](../scripts/task_run/task_run_contract.py),\n[`task_run_scope.py`](../scripts/task_run/task_run_scope.py),\n[`task_run_git.py`](../scripts/task_run/task_run_git.py),\n[`task_run_changed_line.py`](../scripts/task_run/task_run_changed_line.py),\n[`task_run_completion.py`](../scripts/task_run/task_run_completion.py),\n[`task_run_retention.py`](../scripts/task_run/task_run_retention.py), and\n[`runtime_root_retention.py`](../scripts/gates_support/runtime_root_retention.py).\nDo not recopy receipt fields here; `--help` is the typed surface. `--scope`\nrepeats; `--skip-prepare` and `--allow-no-change` are diagnostic opt-outs;\n`--path` and `--branch` are for exceptional host setup; `--base` also\noverrides the lane HEAD base in shorthand.\n\nThe parent reads the receipt before integrating. A lane is done only when\n`changed_line_gate` is `clean` or `noop`. Useful dirty work is committed before\nproof; completion re-observes after the gate and denies approval for dirt, read\nfailure, or changed identity. Retention releases a finished worktree only when\nthe lane-branch commit carries the whole candidate (`commit-only`, clean tree)\nwith a `clean`/`noop` changed-line proof — even when other blockers deny\napproval. Anything else (dirty tree, missing proof, persistence or observation\nfailure) keeps `keep_worktree` true. That flag is a newest-N hold, not a pin:\nthe sweep keeps the newest 10 kept worktrees per key with their `runtime/` dirs\nand removes older ones only after verified salvage; `result.json` and logs\nalways stay. Writes block only when committed or dirty parent paths intersect actual\ncandidate paths; missing candidate evidence refuses completion. Declared\nscope is advisory.\n\n## Detach and wait\n\n```bash\ncharness task run --repo-root . --lane x --detach\ncharness task wait --repo-root . <task-id>... [--any]\n```\n\n`--detach` returns once the carrier has started (exit 0 with the task id\nand result path) or with the launch failure's exit code; `--dry-run` reports\nthe scope refusal with the launch's own code instead of launching. `task\nwait` blocks until the named lanes end and exits with the finished lane's\ncode, so a host background job's notification carries the verdict.\n`--rules-file` names standing material by absolute path (never inlined,\nnever scope evidence); `--grant-writable` grants a codex lane one\nhost-state directory (refused for muse, the repo root, `$HOME`, and `/`).\n\n## Status\n\n```bash\ncharness task status --repo-root .\ncharness task status --repo-root . <task-id>\n```\n\nStatus reads the external result store. Each record adds read-time `liveness`\n(`runner_pid` and advisory `alive`); a dead pid on a `running` record is stale,\nwhile a live pid on a terminal record is normal during retention.\n\nEach JSON line of `<executor>.events.log` gains `charness_received_at` (UTC\nreceive time); `task status <task-id> --commands` reports command\ndurations (receive-time estimates), family starts (pattern matches), and phase times\n([owner](../scripts/task_run/task_run_event_timing.py)). Event byte loss is recorded in `execution.event_capture_incomplete`.\n\n### Aggregate running-task inventory\n\n`charness task status --repo-root <parent> --all-repos` returns the running count across immediate sibling keys in the caller's managed runtime tree, without task-ID prefix filtering or recursive scans. The [runtime owner](../scripts/runtime_bootstrap.py) and [retention owner](../scripts/gates_support/runtime_root_retention.py) derive that boundary. A standalone runtime root reports its narrower scope; neither claims whole-machine coverage.\n\nOnly canonical `task-run/*/result.json` records count. Live running records count as running, dead running records as stale, and known terminal states as terminal even while their runner finishes retention. Unknown status, unreadable or malformed storage, and unconfirmed liveness make completeness partial. Status never repairs records. An admission caller must consume both `completeness` and `running_count`; successful query execution is not admission approval.\n\nComplete schema-bearing JSON/YAML delivery is exposed as `result_delivery.structured`;\nreview-lifecycle input is also projected as `reviewer_lifecycle`. Bounded review\nJSON stays reusable as `reviewer_result`, including partial, deferred, blocked,\ntimed-out, or failed runs, but is not approval until a consumer rebinds its\npacket, input identity, and task receipt. Malformed review JSON is retained as\npartial-schema for retry; prose is not applicable. Terminal records and bounded\nlogs remain available in the external result store.\n",
