    "path": "tests/charness_cli/test_task_run_progress_rule.py",
    "prompt_content": "\"\"\"The lane progress rule, driven on an injected clock and worktree fingerprint.\n\nNo test here reads wall time: `now` is passed in and the worktree state is a\nvalue the test sets, so every verdict follows from the inputs alone.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport subprocess\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom scripts.task_run import task_run_progress_rule as rule_owner\n\nfrom .test_task_run_fixtures import _repo\n\nTEST = \"/usr/bin/zsh -lc 'pnpm run test:changed'\"\n\n\nclass _Tree:\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        self.state: str | None = \"tree-0\"\n\n    def __call__(self) -> str | None:\n        return self.state\n\n\ndef _rule(window: float = 100.0) -> tuple[rule_owner.ProgressRule, _Tree]:\n    tree = _Tree()\n    rule = rule_owner.ProgressRule(window_seconds=window, fingerprint=tree)\n    rule.start(0.0)\n    return rule, tree\n\n\ndef _completed(command: str, item_id: str, exit_code: int = 1) -> dict[str, object]:\n    return {\n        \"type\": \"item.completed\",\n        \"item\": {\n            \"id\": item_id,\n            \"type\": \"command_execution\",\n            \"command\": command,\n            \"exit_code\": exit_code,\n        },\n    }\n\n\ndef test_a_window_with_progress_asks_nothing() -> None:\n    rule, tree = _rule()\n    tree.state = \"tree-1\"  # a file changed\n\n    assert rule.on_tick(99.0) is None  # inside the window: no check yet\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is None\n    assert rule.receipt()[\"checks\"][-1][\"progress\"] is True\n\n\ndef test_an_empty_window_requests_a_checkpoint_and_the_next_one_stops() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n\n    request = rule.on_tick(100.0)\n    assert request is not None and request.kind == \"message\"\n    assert request.reason_code == \"no-progress\"\n    assert \"commit it now\" in request.text and \"never --no-verify\" in request.text\n    assert rule.snapshot(150.0)[\"checkpoint_request\"] == \"delivered\"\n\n    stop = rule.on_tick(200.0)\n    assert stop is not None and stop.kind == \"stop\"\n    assert \"checkpoint request went unanswered\" in stop.text\n    assert rule.receipt()[\"stop_reason\"] == stop.text\n\n\ndef test_answering_the_request_with_any_progress_clears_it() -> None:\n    rule, tree = _rule()\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is not None\n\n    tree.state = \"tree-after-commit\"  # the lane committed\n    assert rule.on_tick(200.0) is None\n    assert rule.snapshot(200.0)[\"checkpoint_request\"] is None\n    assert rule.receipt()[\"messages\"][0][\"answered\"] is True\n    # A later empty window starts the sequence over with a request, not a stop.\n    request = rule.on_tick(300.0)\n    assert request is not None and request.kind == \"message\"\n\n\ndef test_a_new_command_outcome_is_progress_but_a_repeat_is_not() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n    rule.on_event(_completed(\"cat a.py\", \"c1\"))\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is None  # one new outcome\n\n    rule.on_event(_completed(\"cat a.py\", \"c2\"))  # same command, same tree\n    assert rule.on_tick(200.0) is not None  # no new outcome: request\n\n\ndef test_an_unobservable_worktree_falls_back_to_the_executors_own_events() -> None:\n    rule, tree = _rule()\n    tree.state = None\n    edit = {\"type\": \"item.completed\", \"item\": {\"id\": \"f1\", \"type\": \"file_change\"}}\n\n    rule.on_event(edit)\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is None  # the executor reported an edit\n    assert rule.receipt()[\"checks\"][-1][\"tree_observable\"] is False\n    # Silence with an unreadable tree is still silence: request, then stop.\n    assert rule.on_tick(200.0).kind == \"message\"\n    assert rule.on_tick(300.0).kind == \"stop\"\n\n\ndef test_the_checkpoint_names_every_command_the_kill_interrupts() -> None:\n    \"\"\"The kill takes the whole process group, so the message names every\n    running command, not only the last one (follow-up review 24).\"\"\"\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n    for item_id, command in ((\"c1\", TEST), (\"c2\", \"sleep 900\")):\n        rule.on_event({\"type\": \"item.started\",\n                       \"item\": {\"id\": item_id, \"type\": \"command_execution\", \"command\": command}})\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is None  # both young: deferred\n    request = rule.on_tick(200.0)\n    assert request is not None and f\"`{TEST}`\" in request.text and \"`sleep 900`\" in request.text\n\n\ndef test_a_loop_repeat_that_resolves_a_deferred_request_delivers_the_checkpoint() -> None:\n    \"\"\"A third identical test run arriving while a request is deferred sends the\n    checkpoint request, not the loop message, so the state the next check reads\n    matches what the lane received (follow-up review 24).\"\"\"\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n    rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, \"c1\"))\n    rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, \"c2\"))\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is None  # the first run was a new outcome\n    rule.on_event({\"type\": \"item.started\",\n                   \"item\": {\"id\": \"c3\", \"type\": \"command_execution\", \"command\": TEST}})\n    assert rule.on_tick(200.0) is None  # deferred for the young third run\n    action = rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, \"c3\"))\n    assert action is not None and action.reason_code == \"no-progress\"\n    assert rule.snapshot(200.0)[\"checkpoint_request\"] == \"delivered-between-checks\"\n\n\ndef test_a_command_in_flight_defers_the_request_instead_of_killing_it() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n    started = {\"type\": \"item.started\",\n               \"item\": {\"id\": \"c1\", \"type\": \"command_execution\", \"command\": TEST}}\n    rule.on_event(started)\n\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is None  # deferred: the command may be long\n    assert rule.snapshot(100.0)[\"checkpoint_request\"] == \"deferred\"\n    # Still running at the next check: now it is interrupted with the request.\n    request = rule.on_tick(200.0)\n    assert request is not None and request.kind == \"message\"\n    assert \"Every command still running was interrupted\" in request.text\n    assert TEST in request.text\n    assert rule.on_tick(300.0).kind == \"stop\"\n\n\ndef test_a_deferred_request_is_withdrawn_when_the_long_command_finishes() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n    rule.on_event({\"type\": \"item.started\",\n                   \"item\": {\"id\": \"c1\", \"type\": \"command_execution\", \"command\": TEST}})\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is None\n    rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, \"c1\"))  # a new outcome\n\n    assert rule.on_tick(200.0) is None\n    assert rule.snapshot(200.0)[\"checkpoint_request\"] is None\n\n\ndef test_a_deferred_request_is_delivered_at_a_repeat_completion() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n    rule.on_event(_completed(\"cat a.py\", \"c0\"))\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is None  # that outcome was progress\n    rule.on_event({\"type\": \"item.started\",\n                   \"item\": {\"id\": \"c1\", \"type\": \"command_execution\", \"command\": \"cat a.py\"}})\n    assert rule.on_tick(200.0) is None  # deferred\n    request = rule.on_event(_completed(\"cat a.py\", \"c1\"))  # nothing new\n\n    assert request is not None and request.reason_code == \"no-progress\"\n    assert \"interrupted\" not in request.text\n    # Delivered between checks: the next check opens the request's full\n    # window instead of stopping seconds after delivery (claims review C-B1).\n    assert rule.on_tick(300.0) is None\n    assert rule.checks[-1][\"request_window_started\"] is True\n    stop = rule.on_tick(400.0)\n    assert stop is not None and stop.kind == \"stop\"\n\n\ndef test_the_third_identical_test_run_asks_for_debug_then_checkpoint_then_stops() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n\n    assert rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, \"c1\")) is None\n    assert rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, \"c2\")) is None\n    debug = rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, \"c3\", exit_code=1))\n    assert debug is not None and debug.reason_code == \"loop-debug\"\n    assert \"debug skill\" in debug.text and \"last exit code 1\" in debug.text\n    checkpoint = rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, \"c4\"))\n    assert checkpoint is not None and checkpoint.reason_code == \"loop-checkpoint\"\n    stop = rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, \"c5\"))\n    assert stop is not None and stop.kind == \"stop\" and stop.reason_code == \"loop-stop\"\n\n\ndef test_a_file_change_between_runs_resets_the_loop_count() -> None:\n    rule, tree = _rule()\n    for index in range(6):\n        tree.state = f\"tree-{index}\"  # an edit before every run\n        assert rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, f\"c{index}\")) is None\n\n\ndef test_repeats_outside_the_loop_families_never_message() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n    for index in range(6):\n        assert rule.on_event(_completed(\"cat a.py\", f\"c{index}\")) is None\n\n\ndef test_muse_bash_completions_feed_the_same_rule() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n\n    def bash(task_id: str) -> list[dict[str, object]]:\n        chunk = json.dumps({\"command\": \"python3 -m pytest tests/a.py\", \"exit_code\": 1})\n        return [\n            {\"payload_type\": \"task.lifecycle.proposed\",\n             \"payload\": {\"task_id\": task_id, \"event\": {\"task_kind\": \"tool.bash\"}}},\n            {\"payload_type\": \"task.lifecycle.started\", \"payload\": {\"task_id\": task_id}},\n            {\"payload_type\": \"task.lifecycle.output\",\n             \"payload\": {\"task_id\": task_id, \"event\": {\"chunk\": chunk}}},\n            {\"payload_type\": \"task.lifecycle.completed\", \"payload\": {\"task_id\": task_id}},\n        ]\n\n    actions = [rule.on_event(event) for task in (\"t1\", \"t2\", \"t3\") for event in bash(task)]\n    assert [action.reason_code for action in actions if action] == [\"loop-debug\"]\n\n\ndef test_a_non_positive_window_turns_the_stop_off() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule(window=0)\n\n    assert rule.on_tick(10_000.0) is None\n    assert rule.receipt()[\"stop_enabled\"] is False\n    # `0` turns the whole rule off, loop check included (claims review B1).\n    assert [rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, f\"c{n}\")) for n in range(1, 6)] == [None] * 5\n\n\ndef test_worktree_fingerprint_sees_edits_commits_and_untracked_but_not_ignored(\n    tmp_path: Path,\n) -> None:\n    repo = _repo(tmp_path, ignored=True)\n    first = rule_owner.worktree_fingerprint(repo)\n    assert first is not None and rule_owner.worktree_fingerprint(repo) == first\n\n    (repo / \"ignored-output.txt\").write_text(\"cache\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    assert rule_owner.worktree_fingerprint(repo) == first\n\n    (repo / \"module.py\").write_text(\"VALUE = 2\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    edited = rule_owner.worktree_fingerprint(repo)\n    assert edited != first\n\n    (repo / \"new.py\").write_text(\"NEW = 1\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    untracked = rule_owner.worktree_fingerprint(repo)\n    assert untracked != edited\n\n    for args in ((\"add\", \"-A\"), (\"-c\", \"user.email=t@e\", \"-c\", \"user.name=t\", \"commit\", \"-qm\", \"x\")):\n        subprocess.run([\"git\", *args], cwd=repo, check=True)\n    assert rule_owner.worktree_fingerprint(repo) not in {first, edited, untracked}\n    assert rule_owner.worktree_fingerprint(tmp_path / \"missing\") is None\n\n\ndef test_reruns_on_an_unreadable_tree_are_not_progress_and_never_claim_a_loop() -> None:\n    rule, tree = _rule()\n    tree.state = None\n    assert rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, \"c0\")) is None  # new: progress\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is None\n    for index in range(1, 6):\n        assert rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, f\"c{index}\")) is None\n    assert rule.on_tick(200.0).kind == \"message\"\n\n\ndef test_a_command_started_after_the_request_is_not_killed_until_it_is_a_window_old() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0).kind == \"message\"  # delivered\n    rule.on_event({\"type\": \"item.started\",\n                   \"item\": {\"id\": \"late\", \"type\": \"command_execution\", \"command\": TEST}})\n\n    assert rule.on_tick(200.0) is None  # the command is younger than a window\n    assert rule.receipt()[\"checks\"][-1][\"deferred_for_young_command\"] is True\n    stop = rule.on_tick(300.0)  # still running a window later, nothing moved\n    assert stop is not None and stop.kind == \"stop\"\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    (\"ended_normally\", \"answer\"), [(True, (True, \"lane-ended\")), (False, (False, \"lane-failed\"))]\n)\ndef test_only_a_clean_end_answers_a_checkpoint_request(\n    ended_normally: bool, answer: tuple[bool, str]\n) -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0).kind == \"message\"\n    rule.finish(ended_normally=ended_normally)\n\n    message = rule.receipt()[\"messages\"][0]\n    assert (message[\"answered\"], message[\"answered_by\"]) == answer\n    assert rule.snapshot(100.0)[\"checkpoint_request\"] is None\n\n\ndef test_a_resumed_session_reusing_an_item_id_is_a_new_young_command() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Codex numbers items per turn, so `item_1` after a resume is a new command.\"\"\"\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n    started = {\"type\": \"item.started\",\n               \"item\": {\"id\": \"item_1\", \"type\": \"command_execution\", \"command\": \"sleep 300\"}}\n    rule.on_event(started)\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is None  # young: deferred\n    assert rule.on_tick(200.0).kind == \"message\"  # a window old: request, killing it\n    rule.start(210.0)  # the resumed invocation\n    rule.on_event(started)  # the lane restarts it under the same item id\n\n    assert rule.on_tick(300.0) is None\n    assert rule.receipt()[\"checks\"][-1][\"deferred_for_young_command\"] is True\n    assert rule.on_tick(400.0).kind == \"stop\"\n\n\ndef test_a_stopped_rule_raises_no_more_messages() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0).kind == \"message\"\n    assert rule.on_tick(200.0).kind == \"stop\"\n    for index in range(3):\n        assert rule.on_event(_completed(TEST, f\"c{index}\")) is None\n\n\ndef test_muse_edit_tools_are_outcomes_but_model_responses_are_not() -> None:\n    rule, _tree = _rule()\n\n    def task(task_id: str, kind: str) -> list[dict[str, object]]:\n        return [\n            {\"payload_type\": \"task.lifecycle.proposed\",\n             \"payload\": {\"task_id\": task_id, \"event\": {\"task_kind\": kind}}},\n            {\"payload_type\": \"task.lifecycle.completed\", \"payload\": {\"task_id\": task_id}},\n        ]\n\n    for kind in (\"model.meta.response\", \"tool.read_file\", \"tool.search\", \"tool.write_todos\"):\n        for event in task(f\"r-{kind}\", kind):\n            rule.on_event(event)\n    assert rule.on_tick(100.0) is not None  # reading and planning alone are not progress\n    for event in task(\"e1\", \"tool.edit_file\"):\n        rule.on_event(event)\n    assert rule.on_tick(200.0) is None\n\n\ndef test_worktree_fingerprint_is_none_outside_git_and_skips_vanished_files(\n    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch\n) -> None:\n    plain = tmp_path / \"not-a-repo\"\n    plain.mkdir()\n    assert rule_owner.worktree_fingerprint(plain) is None\n\n    repo = _repo(tmp_path)\n    (repo / \"gone.txt\").write_text(\"x\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    import os as real_os\n\n    def vanished(path: object, **kwargs: object) -> object:\n        if str(path).endswith(\"gone.txt\"):\n            raise FileNotFoundError(path)\n        return real_os.stat(path, **kwargs)\n\n    # Rebinds only this module's `os`; the file vanishes between listing and stat.\n    monkeypatch.setattr(rule_owner, \"os\", type(\"Os\", (), {\"stat\": staticmethod(vanished)}))\n    assert rule_owner.worktree_fingerprint(repo) is not None\n\n\ndef test_bootstrap_shims_insert_the_repo_root_when_it_is_absent(\n    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,\n) -> None:\n    import sys\n\n    from scripts.task_run import task_run_lane_signals\n\n    root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\n    for module in (rule_owner, task_run_lane_signals):\n        stripped = [entry for entry in sys.path if entry and Path(entry).resolve() != root]\n        monkeypatch.setattr(sys, \"path\", stripped)\n        module._load_repo_runtime_bootstrap()\n        assert str(root) in sys.path\n",

    "prompt_content": "\"\"\"Self-review receipt states and unavailable-review non-claims.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom scripts.task_run import task_run, task_run_execution, task_run_runtime, task_run_self_review\nfrom tests.charness_cli.test_task_run_fixtures import _codex\n\n\ndef test_self_review_findings_are_grouped_by_disposition() -> None:\n    block = task_run_self_review.self_review_block(\n        \"findings-received\",\n        findings=[\n            {\"summary\": \"The boundary is covered.\", \"disposition\": \"fixed\", \"evidence\": [\"src/a.py:2\"]},\n            {\"summary\": \"The follow-up remains open.\", \"disposition\": \"deferred\", \"evidence\": []},\n            {\"summary\": \"The reported risk does not apply.\", \"disposition\": \"disputed\", \"evidence\": []},\n        ],\n    )\n\n    assert block[\"kind\"] == \"charness.task_self_review.v1\"\n    assert [item[\"summary\"] for item in block[\"findings\"][\"fixed\"]] == [\n        \"The boundary is covered.\"\n    ]\n    assert [item[\"disposition\"] for item in block[\"findings\"][\"deferred\"]] == [\n        \"deferred\"\n    ]\n    assert [item[\"disposition\"] for item in block[\"findings\"][\"disputed\"]] == [\n        \"disputed\"\n    ]\n    assert \"independent review\" in block[\"non_claim\"]\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    (\"state\", \"findings\", \"message\"),\n    [\n        (\"passed\", [], \"unknown self-review state\"),\n        (\"findings-received\", \"invalid\", \"must be a list\"),\n        (\"findings-received\", [{\"summary\": \"risk\", \"disposition\": \"open\"}], \"needs\"),\n        (\"findings-received\", [{\"summary\": \" \", \"disposition\": \"fixed\"}], \"non-empty\"),\n        (\n            \"findings-received\",\n            [{\"summary\": \"risk\", \"disposition\": \"fixed\", \"evidence\": [\"file.py\", 3]}],\n            \"evidence must be a list\",\n        ),\n        (\n            \"findings-received\",\n            [{\"summary\": \"risk\", \"disposition\": \"fixed\"}],\n            \"evidence must be a list\",\n        ),\n    ],\n)\ndef test_self_review_rejects_malformed_findings(state: str, findings: object, message: str) -> None:\n    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match=message):\n        task_run_self_review.self_review_block(state, findings=findings)\n\n\ndef test_self_review_filters_blank_evidence() -> None:\n    block = task_run_self_review.self_review_block(\n        \"findings-received\",\n        findings=[{\"summary\": \"Risk\", \"disposition\": \"fixed\", \"evidence\": [\"path:2\", \" \"]}],\n    )\n\n    assert block[\"findings\"][\"fixed\"][0][\"evidence\"] == [\"path:2\"]\n\n\ndef test_self_review_prompt_and_schema_use_list_evidence() -> None:\n    schema = task_run_self_review.self_review_schema()\n    finding_schema = schema[\"properties\"][\"findings\"][\"items\"]\n\n    assert schema[\"required\"] == list(schema[\"properties\"])\n    assert schema[\"properties\"][\"kind\"][\"enum\"] == [\"charness.task_self_review.v1\"]\n    assert finding_schema[\"properties\"][\"evidence\"] == {\n        \"type\": \"array\", \"items\": {\"type\": \"string\"}\n    }\n    prompt = task_run_self_review.self_review_prompt(\"Review.\", \"base\", [\"file.py\"])\n    assert finding_schema[\"required\"] == list(finding_schema[\"properties\"])\n    assert \"minLength\" not in json.dumps(schema)\n    assert \"use [] when there is no evidence\" in prompt\n    assert \"optional string evidence\" not in prompt\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    (\"executor\", \"command\", \"reason_fragment\"),\n    [\n        (\"muse\", [\"/not-used/muse\"], \"read-only Codex reviewer is unavailable\"),\n        (\"codex\", None, \"reviewer command is unavailable\"),\n        (\"codex\", [\"/not-installed/codex\"], \"reviewer executable is unavailable\"),\n    ],\n)\ndef test_unavailable_self_review_is_a_reasoned_non_claim(\n    tmp_path: Path,\n    executor: str,\n    command: list[str] | None,\n    reason_fragment: str,\n) -> None:\n    block = task_run_execution.run_self_review(\n        {\n            \"self_review_policy\": \"always\",\n            \"_self_review_prompt\": \"Review this candidate.\",\n            \"executor\": {\"kind\": executor, \"command\": command},\n            \"candidate\": {\"changed_paths\": [\"src/module.py\"]},\n            \"execution_runtime_root\": str(tmp_path / \"runtime\"),\n            \"worktree_path\": str(tmp_path),\n        }\n    )\n\n    assert block[\"state\"] == \"unavailable-skip\"\n    assert reason_fragment in block[\"reason\"]\n    assert \"not review-passed\" in block[\"non_claim\"]\n    assert block[\"findings\"] == {\"fixed\": [], \"deferred\": [], \"disputed\": []}\n\n\ndef test_self_review_normalizes_runtime_failures(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    blocked_runtime = tmp_path.parent / f\"{tmp_path.name}-runtime-file\"\n    blocked_runtime.write_text(\"file\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    runtime_failure = task_run_execution.run_self_review(\n        {\n            \"self_review_policy\": \"always\",\n            \"_self_review_prompt\": \"Review this candidate.\",\n            \"executor\": {\"kind\": \"codex\", \"command\": [sys.executable]},\n            \"execution_runtime_root\": str(blocked_runtime),\n            \"worktree_path\": str, worktree: bool, suffix: str\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Parallel lanes counted files in their own worktrees, picked the same\n    `followup-N`, and collided on merge (ceal int208-int210, 2026-10-20). A lane\n    branch names the record; a default branch or detached HEAD keeps the counter.\"\"\"\n    repo = tmp_path / \"repo\"\n    _debug_dir, target = _seed_resolved_debug_pointer(repo)\n    _seed_git_head(repo, head, worktree=worktree)\n\n    payload = _scaffold_debug.payload_for(repo, title=None)\n\n    assert payload[\"write_artifact_path\"].endswith(suffix)\n    assert payload[\"update_current_pointer_after_write\"] is True\n    assert payload[\"current_pointer_symlink_target\"] == target.name\n\n\ndef test_debug_followup_lane_names_are_distinct_across_aliasing_branches(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"A lossy slug let `task/foo_bar` and `task/foo-bar` (or `Foo` and `foo`)\n    pick the same record, a plain `foo-bar` aliased `task/foo-bar`, and a task\n    id spelled as another branch's hashed token aliased that branch; every\n    lane token now carries a hash of its own raw branch, so only an 8-hex\n    hash-prefix collision could still alias two branches.\"\"\"\n    names = set()\n    plain = \"foo-bar-\" + hashlib.sha256(b\"foo-bar\").hexdigest()[:8]\n    branches = (\n        \"task/foo-bar\", \"task/foo_bar\", \"task/Foo-bar\", \"task/foo.bar\", \"task/__\", \"foo-bar\",\n        f\"task/{plain}\",\n    )\n    for index, branch in enumerate(branches):\n        repo = tmp_path / f\"repo{index}\"\n        _seed_resolved_debug_pointer(repo)\n        _seed_git_head(repo, f\"ref: refs/heads/{branch}\", worktree=True)\n        names.add(Path(_scaffold_debug.payload_for(repo, title=None)[\"write_artifact_path\"]).name)\n    assert len(names) == len(branches)\n    assert all(\"-debug-review-followup-\" in name for name in names)\n    assert any(\"-debug-review-followup-branch-\" in name for name in names)  # task/__\n\n\ndef test_a_lane_branch_never_takes_the_plain_dated_name(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Two lanes routed off a resolved pointer whose target is not today's\n    default name both took `<date>-debug-review.md` (claims review C-A3).\"\"\"\n    repo = tmp_path / \"repo\"\n    _seed_debug_adapter(repo)\n    debug_dir = repo / \"charness-artifacts\" / \"debug\"\n    debug_dir.mkdir(parents=True)\n    target = debug_dir / \"debug-2026-05-06-demo.md\"\n    target.write_text(\n        \"# Demo Debug\\n\\n## Interrupt Decision\\n\\n- Resolution: resolved\\n\\n## Problem\\n\\nTODO\\n\",\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n    )\n    (debug_dir / \"latest.md\").symlink_to(target.name)\n    _seed_git_head(repo, \"ref: refs/heads/task/lane-a\", worktree=True)\n\n    name = Path(_scaffold_debug.payload_for(repo, title=None)[\"write_artifact_path\"]).name\n\n    digest = hashlib.sha256(b\"task/lane-a\").hexdigest()[:8]\n    assert name.endswith(f\"-debug-review-followup-lane-a-{digest}.md\")\n\n\ndef test_a_reftable_placeholder_head_is_no_lane_identity(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    repo = tmp_path / \"repo\"\n    _seed_resolved_debug_pointer(repo)\n    _seed_git_head(repo, \"ref: refs/heads/.invalid\", worktree=False)\n\n    payload = _scaffold_debug.payload_for(repo, title=None)\n\n    assert payload[\"write_artifact_path\"].endswith(\"-debug-review-followup.md\")\n\n\ndef test_debug_followup_unreadable_git_link_keeps_the_counter(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"A `.git` file that is not a gitdir link gives no lane identity.\"\"\"\n    repo = tmp_path / \"repo\"\n    _seed_resolved_debug_pointer(repo)\n    (repo / \".git\").write_text(\"not a git link\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n    payload = _scaffold_debug.payload_for(repo, title=None)\n\n    assert payload[\"write_artifact_path\"].endswith(\"-debug-review-followup.md\")\n",
    "prompt_encoding": "utf-8",
    "size_bytes": 16770,
    "source": "working-tree:tests/test_debug_scaffold.py"
  }

    "prior_findings": [
      {
        "action": "Preserve both actions or advance the checkpoint state only when the checkpoint is actually delivered. Add a regression test for a third or fourth loop completion that also resolves a deferred request.",
        "evidence": [
          "In `ProgressRule._on_complete`, a repeated command completing after a deferred check sets `_request` to `delivered-between-checks` and `_pending` to the checkpoint action.",
          "For the third or fourth identical test, lint, or typecheck completion, the loop branch overwrites `_pending` with its own message. `on_event` delivers only that final action.",
          "The next empty check treats `delivered-between-checks` as an actual checkpoint delivery and starts its window; the lane can therefore be stopped for an unanswered checkpoint it never received.",
          "The added C-B1 test uses `cat a.py`, outside the loop families, so it does not cover this overlap."
        ],
        "id": "C-B1-loop-action-overwrites-checkpoint",
        "severity": "high",
        "summary": "A loop message can replace a deferred checkpoint request while the rule still records that checkpoint as delivered."
      },
      {
        "action": "Name all in-flight commands that may be interrupted, or make the message and spec explicitly warn that any running commands may be interrupted and have partial effects.",
        "evidence": [
          "`ProgressRule._in_flight` tracks multiple commands, while `on_tick` selects only `list(in_flight.values())[-1]` for the interruption warning.",
          "`_kill_recorded_process_group` kills the executor's process group, which can include other running commands.",
          "The spec says the interruption message names the command and warns about its partial effects; the generated request tells the lane not to simply rerun the named command. Other interrupted commands can have unreported partial effects."
        ],
        "id": "parallel-command-interrupt-not-disclosed",
        "severity": "high",
        "summary": "A window-triggered preemption can interrupt multiple in-flight commands, but the checkpoint message identifies only one."
      }
    ],
    "prior_lens": "release lock-in final pass",
    "prior_next_move": "Hold release lock-in until checkpoint delivery state is corrected for overlapping loop actions and the interruption warning covers concurrent commands; add focused regression coverage.",

      "title": "Non-Goals For This Contract"
    },
    {
      "content": "# Reviewer-Packet Semantic Question\n\nUse this question when a slice changes a guard, reference, claim, or verdict\nsurface. It keeps a reviewer packet anchored to what a reader or control must\nknow, rather than to the observable form that happened to expose the problem.\n\n## Ask Before Broad Sampling\n\nThe packet author and reviewer should use all four parts when they apply. If a\npart is not applicable or cannot be established, record `not applicable` or\n`insufficient evidence` with the reason; do not silently claim the control is\nproven.\n\n1. **Semantic fact or invariant:** what must be true, independently of the\n   current representation or failure spelling?\n2. **Owning boundary:** which source, helper, renderer, reference, or workflow\n   boundary carries or derives that fact, and who reads it?\n3. **Recorded instance:** which concrete observed instance must this slice catch,\n   explain, or preserve?\n4. **Axis-varying counterexample:** what changes the semantic axis while keeping\n   the observed form similar enough to expose a proxy-based control?\n\nThe question is a review aid, not a packet-readiness predicate. A clean tree is\nnot evidence that the selected control catches a recorded instance.\n\n## Compare the Proposed Control\n\nAfter naming the four parts, state the proposed predicate, claim, or surface\nchange and compare it with the counterexample:\n\n- If the observed form changes while the semantic fact does not, reject or\n  repair a control that changes its verdict with that form.\n- If the semantic fact changes while the observed form stays similar, reject or\n  repair a control that cannot distinguish the changed outcome.\n- If the comparison cannot be made, record `unproven — defer`; do not approve it\n  as though a clean-tree result were proof.\n- For a behavior-changing helper or command, first record the bounded candidate\n  search and scope. When that change has a reader-facing or copy-paste reference\n  in scope, identify the first reader and verify that its demonstrated invocation\n  preserves the claimed behavior. Disposition each discovered reference as\n  updated, not applicable, or insufficient evidence with a reason. If no such\n  reference is in scope, record `not applicable` with the search scope; if the\n  reader cannot be checked, record `insufficient evidence` or `unproven — defer`\n  rather than treating the helper's own tests as proof of reference safety.\n\nThese are reviewer dispositions, not an automated semantic gate.\n\n## Decision Boundary\n\n- Prefer a surface fix when the owning surface can carry or derive the semantic\n  fact and prove the recorded instance.\n- Keep the control as a reviewer question when the fact is judgment-bound or\n  cannot be mechanically observed without guessing.\n- Add a gate only when the predicate is mechanically observable, its false-fire\n  cost is understood, and a recorded escape supports the addition.\n\nThis is a reviewer question, not a semantic meta-gate. It does not claim that a\nhost renders the packet, that a reviewer reaches the right judgment, or that a\nclean-tree run proves the control.\n",
      "content_kind": "static",
